Analysis summary:

  • Design: 5 M vs 5 WT; group labels from file names (assumption stated). n=5/group, two-group moderated t-test (limma eBayes port), BH.
  • Matrix: 1794 features × 10 samples; 590 tested (≥2 detections in both groups); 371 significant (BH<0.05, |log2FC|>1): 178 up in M, 193 up in WT.
  • PCA: PC1 38.4% complete separation (M −15 to −27 vs WT +20 to +28); PC2 16.4% splits M03/M04 (the low-detection pair).
  • Detection depth: M 488–675 features/sample; WT 1161–1287. → major caveat: WT-only presence (796 features) is confounded with depth; M-only 196.
  • Tier A 182, B 87, C 102.

  • LBA: 59 significant of 81 tested; gene-level summed intensity only −0.36 log2 (i.e., ~0.78×), but proteoform classes move opposite: unshifted down (median −1.78; 24 up WT vs 6 up M), shifted up (median +2.19; 17 up M vs 12 up WT). LGB1: gene-level −1.07; unshifted −1.91, shifted +2.22.

  • 19 of 168 genes have significant features in both directions → proteoform-level remodeling, not gene abundance.

Judgment — what’s worth deeper investigation (ranked):

  1. The +317.39 N-terminal shift cluster on the EKN/100527608 early-nodulin-like pair — unexplained chemistry, extreme effect, recurrence, group-polarized. → MS2 inspection/localization, candidate chemistry, check whether it’s one modification or a series (317/331/341/343/345/434), and whether the series is a truncation-model artifact. (tdp-copilot route; spectral-evidence for fragment support.)
  2. The shifted-vs-unshifted direction split (proteoform-state remodeling) — with LBA/LGB1 as the flagship. Test whether it holds at tier A only (control for detection depth), and whether it’s driven by specific families (+16 oxidation?) or a global search-assignment bias in sparser M data.
  3. fixG (2 features, +6.6, 2/5 vs 5/5) and 100777927_20 (−6.23, 4/4, essentially WT-specific) — strong on/off candidates at low occupancy; verify spectra.
  4. Chaperonin (groL/groS) opposite directions — biologically coherent (GroEL/GroES), worth checking subunit-level quantification; but could be an inference/assignment issue since groL/groS peptides are highly similar.
  5. Not worth deep follow-up without more evidence: the bulk of the 371 significant features (abundance-driven, no shift), and WT-only presence (796 features) which is confounded with detection depth.

Also: the M vs WT detection depth difference itself is worth a technical look — is it sample amount or biology? That’s a QC follow-up.

What I ran

The soybean-all Run folder holds 10 soybean-nodule top-down runs (5 × M01–M05, 5 × WT01–WT05) with TopPIC PrSM/proteoform tables and TopFD MS1 features. I built the quantitative matrix with toppicr-py, then tested M vs WT.

Assumption (user-owned): group membership comes only from the sample-name prefixes — no design file is present. Group A = WT, group B = M, so positive log2FC = higher in M. If that label mapping is wrong, every direction below flips.

  • Matrix: 1794 features × 10 samples, 0 warnings. Group labels confirmed against samples.csv.
  • Test: default paper chain (log2 + feature centering + median polish + RT lowess; two-group OLS + limma-eBayes Python port), BH FDR < 0.05 and |log2FC| > 1. 590 features testable (≥2 detections in both groups); 371 significant (178 higher in M, 193 higher in WT).

Headline numbers

Quantity Value
Testable / significant 590 / 371 (median |log2FC| 2.19)
Tier A / B / C (≥4, 3, ≤2 detections in weaker group) 182 / 87 / 102
PCA PC1 38.4% separates groups completely (M −27.5…−15.0; WT +20.1…+27.6); PC2 16.4% isolates M03/M04
Features per sample M 488–675 (median 665); WT 1161–1287 (median 1264)
Presence-only (untested) 796 WT-only, 196 M-only, 239 partial
Features carrying a numeric shift 592, of which 132 significant; 16 families ≥3 features, 15 recur on ≥2 proteins

The automatic report (9/9 sections ready) — inline copy below; only the Feature-map tab is subsampled for size:

Full version:

Differential views

Reading: the biggest separations are not the best-supported ones — of the top 20 |log2FC| features, 9 are tier A, 3 tier B and 8 tier C.

Reading: within one gene, shifted and unshifted proteoforms move in opposite directions — LBA 17 vs 12 (shifted) and 6 vs 24 (unshifted); the same holds genome-wide and in tier A alone (50/14 vs 49/69), so it is not only a detection-depth effect.

Reading: shift values recur across unrelated proteins (+16.00 Da on 8 proteins, −2.00 Da on 6, +31.02 Da on LBA/LGB1), i.e. shared chemistry or a systematic assignment — both investigable.

Strongest individual effects

Feature Gene log2FC FDR WT / M detections Tier
EKN_3 EKN (early nodulin) +8.92 1.9e-4 3 / 5 B
LBA_186 LBA (leghemoglobin A) +7.04 4.4e-5 2 / 4 C
fixG_1 fixG (Fe-S cluster, Bradyrhizobium) +6.67 8.8e-6 2 / 5 C
100527608_18 uncharacterized, EKN-homologous +6.57 1.0e-6 5 / 5 A
blr7436_3 blr7436 (Bradyrhizobium) +6.33 1.5e-4 5 / 4 A
100777927_20 uncharacterized −6.23 2.5e-4 4 / 4 A

What is worth deeper investigation (ranked)

1. The +317.39 Da N-terminal shift family on EKN and 100527608 — top priority. It combines all three priority signals: chemistry unresolved, extreme separation (max |log2FC| 8.92, the single largest in the comparison), and recurrence on two proteins. All four tested features are higher in M (3 significant, 0 in WT). MS2 support is genuinely strong (8–55 matched fragments, E down to 1e-34), and the same species is identified in WT02/WT03/WT04 at low abundance — so this is an abundance difference, not a detection artefact, while the chemistry remains open. Two observations to carry forward: a form modeled with initiator-Met removal carries +448.4332 Da = +317.39 + 131.04 (Met), i.e. the residual tracks the terminal-form model; and one M03 PrSM carries +316.3849 Da (~1.006 Da lower, possible isotope registration). Neighbouring values (+331.41, +341.39, +343.41, +345.42, +434.42) on the same two proteins mean “one modification” is not established. Next: fragment coverage over the N-terminal span, then candidate mass models.

2. Proteoform-state remodelling, with leghemoglobin as the flagship. LBA has 59/81 testable features significant and LGB1 21/23, yet summed intensity barely moves (log2 −0.36 and −1.07). What changes is the distribution: unshifted leghemoglobin proteoforms fall (median −1.78) while shifted ones rise (+2.19). 19 of 168 significant genes carry features in both directions. Worth pursuing because it redefines the biology as modification-state switching rather than protein abundance — but it needs a control for differential shift-assignment rate between deep WT and sparse M data before it is called biological.

3. High-support on/off candidates. 100777927_20 (WT 43–54 Mcps in 4/4, M ≈ 0) and blr7436_3 (136 spectra, 5/4) are clean, well-detected single-proteoform differences; fixG (2/5 vs 5/5) and LBA_186 (2/4) are equally large but tier C, so they need spectra before any claim.

4. Chaperonin direction split. groL3 (15/15), groL6 (6/6) and groL (4/4) all higher in WT, while groS is 9/11 higher in M — coherent GroEL/GroES biology, but groL/groS paralogue inference is a real alternative explanation.

5. The detection-depth asymmetry itself (M ≈ half the features at comparable total intensity). Until this is explained technically, the 796 WT-only features must be treated as not investigated, not as losses.

Not worth pursuing as-is: the bulk of unshifted WT-higher features (depth-confounded) and generic abundance changes without shift or occupancy support.

Limits

Presence-only and low-occupancy features sit outside the volcano; zero means not detected. The 371/590 significance rate is high for a filtered testable subset. M03/M04 separate on PC2 and detect fewest features (possible technical structure within M). Features are clustered proteoform features sharing peptides, so their evidence is correlated, not independent. Let me write the reply now.

Answer

EKN’s +317.3906 shift is not an uncharacterised modification — it is a two-component lipid modification: the protein is N-myristoylated on Gly2 and S-palmitoylated on Cys4, with the initiator Met removed. The search could never have said this: the run allowed one unexpected modification (Maximum number of unexpected modifications: 1), so two acyl chains appear as a single summed residual.

Component Mass (Da) Where it sits Status
Myristoyl (C14H26O) 210.19837 N-terminal α-amine of Gly2 by difference + prior (b1/b2 unobserved)
Palmitoyl (C16H30O) 238.22967 Cys4 (processed Cys3) measured (y-ions, 2 spectra)
Met removal −131.04049 terminal form NME forced by the balance
Sum 448.42803 closes at +0.005 Da on the M02 precursor

How it was established

1. Priors (before any mass search). UniProt Q2KMJ4: 101 aa, disordered, zero modification annotations; dbPTM and iPTMnet empty; BLAST top hits 29–38 % identity to coiled-coil proteins (no usable transfer). ELM returned 17 classes, and one matters: MOD_NMyristoyl matches residues 1–7, MGNCGSN — N-myristoylation requires the free α-amine of Gly2, i.e. Met removal — and Cys4 is the only cysteine in the protein, a competent S-acylation site. Lanes: uniprot hit, motif hit, direct/dbPTM/iPTMnet empty, cohort skipped (no grounded pattern).

2. The mass does not close as one component. unimod-solve over 317.3906 ± 0.02 Da: 0 single matches, 0 two-component combinations (67,303 pruned, none closed). So the NONE-frame residual — the number the search reported — has no chemistry at all.

3. The frame arithmetic recovers it. 24 EKN rows span 7 sequence ranges and 4 terminal models. The same species appears as +317.3901 (NONE), +448.4335 (NME) and +406.4222 (NME + acetylation) — exactly 131.04 Da (Met) and 42.01 Da (acetyl) apart. Solving 448.4335: myristoyl + palmitoyl (−0.0055), spermine + Tween80 (−0.0068), Lys-loss + diacylglycerol (−0.0167). Only the first has site chemistry, and both components are unique UniMod hits at ±0.02 Da (myristoyl specificity includes “any N-term G”; palmitoyl includes Cys). Nineteen of 24 rows close within +0.001…+0.008 Da (0.06–0.7 ppm), and the refined MS1 envelope (11382.8562, Pearson 0.995) lands 0.008 Da from the model.

4. Spectra place the palmitoyl on Cys4. Comparing the model against the same total mass placed entirely at the N-terminus (shared backbone, so ions are separable): b3:1 and b4:1 are degenerate matches — both models predict the same m/z — so the whole 448.43 Da lies inside residues 1–4. The y-ions that start after Met differ by 238.23 and match only the dual-acylation model:

Ion covers Δm/z between models dual acyl N-terminal-mass alternative
y50:6 2–51 39.7 matched, 2.0 ppm, r=0.87 missingApex
y49:5 3–51 47.6 matched, 2.3 ppm correlationRejected
y50:5 / y49:4 (scan 11832) 2–51 / 3–51 47.6 / 59.6 matched missingApex

This replicates on the independent second spectrum (scan 11832, z=5, 34 fragments; coverage 0.92 vs 0.88, median 1.88 vs 1.93 ppm):

The site chain is tight: b3 confines the whole mass to residues 1–4; y49 requires 238.23 at residue ≥3; only Cys4 (processed Cys3) is chemically competent there; residue 2 is Asn, leaving Gly2’s α-amine for the 210.198 remainder — myristoyl, the class the ELM motif predicted.

The other values in the family

The ±CH2 ladder around the di-acyl mass is itself evidence for lipids rather than a search artefact:

Shift (NONE frame) NME-frame mass Closure Δ
+317.39 (19 of 24 rows) 448.433 myristoyl + palmitoyl +0.005
+345.42 476.464 2 × palmitoyl −0.005
+341.39 472.437 2 × palmitoleyl −0.009
+331.40 462.445 no 2-component closure (consistent with an odd-chain acyl absent from UniMod) open
+316.38 — same backbone, raw precursor at 317.40 1 Da isotope-adjustment artefact, not a species

The homolog 100527608 (C6T4V4), whose first 12 residues are identical (MGNCGSNPKTNE), closes on the same model in 23 of 37 rows and shows the same ±14 Da variants.

What this means for the differential finding

EKN_3 (log2FC +8.92, 3/5 vs 5/5) is this dual-acylated full-length proteoform. Every N-terminally intact EKN identification carries the modification — only C-terminal fragments (59–101, 31–52) are unmodified and cannot carry it. So the mutant is not changing acylation stoichiometry; it accumulates the acylated EKN proteoform ~500-fold relative to WT. The same holds for the 100527608 proteoform (+6.57).

Open, and what would settle it

  • The N-terminal residue/acyl is inferred, not observed. b1 (268.227) and b2 (382.270) were absent from every measured spectrum; small N-terminal b-ions are often weak in HCD, so this is a limitation, not a contradiction. An EThcD/ETD spectrum of the 1–52 species, or an MS3 on the low-mass region, would show whether the first residue is myristoyl-Gly.
  • The +331.40 member has no UniMod closure at two components; a custom odd-chain acyl (C15/C17) is the obvious candidate but unverified.
  • Acylation state cannot be compared between groups from these data: no unacylated N-terminal EKN form was identified in either group.
